# Proposed OEIS comments: A219382 to A222890

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A219382

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A219382>  
Result obtained: **8 September 2026**  
Paper: [1478](../papers/01001-01500/01478-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = (1/24)*n^4 - (1/4)*n^3 + (35/24)*n^2 + (15/4)*n - 9 for n>5.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jul 26 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical closed form is correct. The count is a walk count in a finite graph, so it satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence; the conjectured form satisfies the same one, and the two agree at enough consecutive indices to agree from then on, for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A219398

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A219398>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [1266](../papers/01001-01500/01266-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) - 2*a(n-2) + 3*a(n-3) + 4*a(n-4) + 2*a(n-5) - 5*a(n-6) - 2*a(n-7) for n>8.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(4 - 8*x + 12*x^2 - 9*x^3 - 3*x^4 - 7*x^5 + 7*x^6 + 2*x^7) / (1 - 3*x + 2*x^2 - 3*x^3 - 4*x^4 - 2*x^5 + 5*x^6 + 2*x^7). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 11 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 17 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 8. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A220563

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A220563>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3985](../papers/03501-04000/03985-PROOF.pdf)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 5 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A221394

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A221394>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4349](../papers/04001-04500/04349-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 48*a(n-2) - 440*a(n-4) + 998*a(n-6) - 873*a(n-8) + 252*a(n-10) + 16*a(n-12).
> Empirical g.f.: x^2*(18 - 233*x^2 + 820*x^4 - 1050*x^6 + 412*x^8 + 46*x^10) / ((1 - x)*(1 + x)*(1 - 47*x^2 + 393*x^4 - 605*x^6 + 268*x^8 + 16*x^10)). - _Colin Barker_, Aug 05 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 348 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A222099

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A222099>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [10230](../papers/10001-10500/10230-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 42*a(n-1) - 302*a(n-2) + 521*a(n-3) + 114*a(n-4) - 144*a(n-5).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(8 - 71*x + 133*x^2 + 26*x^3 - 36*x^4) / (1 - 42*x + 302*x^2 - 521*x^3 - 114*x^4 + 144*x^5). - _Colin Barker_, Aug 15 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is carried by a bounded amount of state down the array -- a window of three consecutive lines where it names only cells one step apart, and otherwise a row sum together with one flag per adjacent column pair -- so counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 7 states, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is carried by a bounded amount of state down the array -- a window of three consecutive lines where it names only cells one step apart, and otherwise a row sum together with one flag per adjacent column pair -- so counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 5 states, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
